LM-based agents equipped with planning, memory,\n+  and code execution capabilities have the potential to support data-driven science.\n+  However, evaluating agents on such open-ended tasks is challenging due to multiple\n+  valid approaches, partially correct steps, and different ways to express the same\n+  decisions. To address these challenges, we present BLADE, a benchmark to automatically\n+  evaluate agents’ multifaceted approaches to openended research questions. BLADE\n+  consists of 12 datasets and research questions drawn from existing scientific literature,\n+  with ground truth collected from independent analyses by expert data scientists\n+  and researchers. To automatically evaluate agent responses, we developed corresponding\n+  computational methods to match different representations of analyses to this ground\n+  truth. Though language models possess considerable world knowledge, our evaluation\n+  shows that they are often limited to basic analyses. However, agents capable of\n+  interacting with the underlying data demonstrate improved, but still non-optimal,\n+  diversity in their analytical decision making. Our work enables the evaluation of\n+  agents for data-driven science and provides researchers deeper insights into agents’\n+  analysis approaches.\n+author: Ken Gu, Ruoxi Shang, Ruien Jiang, Keying Kuang, Richard-John Lin, Donghe Lyu,\n+  Yue Mao, Youran Pan, Teng Wu, Jiaqian Yu, Yikun Zhang, Tianmai M. 2024 - Transforming Wearable Data into Health Insights us.pdf}\n }\n \n+\n+\n+@online{guBLADEBenchmarkingLanguage2024a,\n+  title = {{{BLADE}}: {{Benchmarking Language Model Agents}} for {{Data-Driven Science}}},\n+  shorttitle = {{{BLADE}}},\n+  author = {Gu, Ken and Shang, Ruoxi and Jiang, Ruien and Kuang, Keying and Lin, Richard-John and Lyu, Donghe and Mao, Yue and Pan, Youran and Wu, Teng and Yu, Jiaqian and Zhang, Yikun and Zhang, Tianmai M. and Zhu, Lanyi and Merrill, Mike A. and Heer, Jeffrey and Althoff, Tim},\n+  date = {2024-08-20},\n+  eprint = {2408.09667},\n+  eprinttype = {arXiv},\n+  eprintclass = {cs},\n+  url = {http://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09667},\n+  urldate = {2024-09-30},\n+  abstract = {Data-driven scientific discovery requires the iterative integration of scientific domain knowledge, statistical expertise, and an understanding of data semantics to make nuanced analytical decisions, e.g., about which variables, transformations, and statistical models to consider. LM-based agents equipped with planning, memory, and code execution capabilities have the potential to support data-driven science. However, evaluating agents on such open-ended tasks is challenging due to multiple valid approaches, partially correct steps, and different ways to express the same decisions. To address these challenges, we present BLADE, a benchmark to automatically evaluate agents’ multifaceted approaches to openended research questions. BLADE consists of 12 datasets and research questions drawn from existing scientific literature, with ground truth collected from independent analyses by expert data scientists and researchers. To automatically evaluate agent responses, we developed corresponding computational methods to match different representations of analyses to this ground truth. LM-based agents equipped with planning, memory,\n+  and code execution capabilities have the potential to support data-driven science.\n+  However, evaluating agents on such open-ended tasks is challenging due to multiple\n+  valid approaches, partially correct steps, and different ways to express the same\n+  decisions. To address these challenges, we present BLADE, a benchmark to automatically\n+  evaluate agents’ multifaceted approaches to openended research questions. BLADE\n+  consists of 12 datasets and research questions drawn from existing scientific literature,\n+  with ground truth collected from independent analyses by expert data scientists\n+  and researchers. To automatically evaluate agent responses, we developed corresponding\n+  computational methods to match different representations of analyses to this ground\n+  truth. Though language models possess considerable world knowledge, our evaluation\n+  shows that they are often limited to basic analyses. However, agents capable of\n+  interacting with the underlying data demonstrate improved, but still non-optimal,\n+  diversity in their analytical decision making. Our work enables the evaluation of\n+  agents for data-driven science and provides researchers deeper insights into agents’\n+  analysis approaches.\n+author: Ken Gu, Ruoxi Shang, Ruien Jiang, Keying Kuang, Richard-John Lin, Donghe Lyu,\n+  Yue Mao, Youran Pan, Teng Wu, Jiaqian Yu, Yikun Zhang, Tianmai M. However, agents capable of\n+  interacting with the underlying data demonstrate improved, but still non-optimal,\n+  diversity in their analytical decision making. However, evaluating agents on such open-ended tasks is challenging due to multiple valid approaches, partially correct steps, and different ways to express the same decisions. To address these challenges, we present BLADE, a benchmark to automatically evaluate agents’ multifaceted approaches to openended research questions. LM-based agents equipped with planning, memory,\n+  and code execution capabilities have the potential to support data-driven science.\n+  However, evaluating agents on such open-ended tasks is challenging due to multiple\n+  valid approaches, partially correct steps, and different ways to express the same\n+  decisions. To address these challenges, we present BLADE, a benchmark to automatically\n+  evaluate agents’ multifaceted approaches to openended research questions. BLADE\n+  consists of 12 datasets and research questions drawn from existing scientific literature,\n+  with ground truth collected from independent analyses by expert data scientists\n+  and researchers. To automatically evaluate agent responses, we developed corresponding\n+  computational methods to match different representations of analyses to this ground\n+  truth. Though language models possess considerable world knowledge, our evaluation\n+  shows that they are often limited to basic analyses. However, agents capable of\n+  interacting with the underlying data demonstrate improved, but still non-optimal,\n+  diversity in their analytical decision making. Our work enables the evaluation of\n+  agents for data-driven science and provides researchers deeper insights into agents’\n+  analysis approaches.\n+author: Ken Gu, Ruoxi Shang, Ruien Jiang, Keying Kuang, Richard-John Lin, Donghe Lyu,\n+  Yue Mao, Youran Pan, Teng Wu, Jiaqian Yu, Yikun Zhang, Tianmai M. LM-based agents equipped with planning, memory,\n+  and code execution capabilities have the potential to support data-driven science.\n+  However, evaluating agents on such open-ended tasks is challenging due to multiple\n+  valid approaches, partially correct steps, and different ways to express the same\n+  decisions. To address these challenges, we present BLADE, a benchmark to automatically\n+  evaluate agents’ multifaceted approaches to openended research questions. BLADE\n+  consists of 12 datasets and research questions drawn from existing scientific literature,\n+  with ground truth collected from independent analyses by expert data scientists\n+  and researchers. To automatically evaluate agent responses, we developed corresponding\n+  computational methods to match different representations of analyses to this ground\n+  truth. Though language models possess considerable world knowledge, our evaluation\n+  shows that they are often limited to basic analyses. However, agents capable of\n+  interacting with the underlying data demonstrate improved, but still non-optimal,\n+  diversity in their analytical decision making. Our work enables the evaluation of\n+  agents for data-driven science and provides researchers deeper insights into agents’\n+  analysis approaches.\n+author: Ken Gu, Ruoxi Shang, Ruien Jiang, Keying Kuang, Richard-John Lin, Donghe Lyu,\n+  Yue Mao, Youran Pan, Teng Wu, Jiaqian Yu, Yikun Zhang, Tianmai M.
